--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I'll analyze the mission and design 14 FSMs for the aggregation task.

**Mission Analysis:**
- Score increases by 1 for every robot OUTSIDE the target region (BLUE-bordered strip with BLACK floor)
- Target: get robots INTO the black-floored strip near blue walls
- Robots start in the center (gray floor)
- Key perceptions: floor color (black = target strip OR green strip), blue color perception

**Strategy:**
- Detect blue walls → move toward blue color (GoToColor with blue)
- When on black floor → stay or aggregate
- Use floor color (black=0) to know when in target zone
- Use neighbor count to aggregate

**Champion analysis:** Score 16884.88 - uses Exploration + ZigZag with neighbor/gray-floor transitions. Not great - robots need to find the blue strip specifically.

**Key insight:** GoToColor with blue (clr=3 might be blue, let me check - colors 1-6, blue walls are BLUE) should navigate toward blue walls directly. When on black floor near blue walls, stop or aggregate.

**My designs:**
1. Survive champion
2-4. Small edits to champion
5-14. New topologies using GoToColor(blue), floor-black detection, attraction

## Reasoning

The new champion is elegant: GoToColor(blue) → when on black floor, Stop; Stop → if gray floor (left zone) go back to GoToColor, or small random chance to restart.

Score dropped from 16884 to 7989 - huge improvement! The key insight is:
- State 0: GoToColor blue (clr=3) - navigate toward blue walls
- State 1: Stop - when on black floor (near blue walls), stay there
- Transition back from Stop if on gray (left the zone) or tiny random reset

**Exploitation:** Tweak probabilities, try Attraction instead of Stop to aggregate robots together, adjust parameters.

**Exploration:** 
- Add a 3rd state for attraction once on black floor

- Use neighbor conditions to consolidate group

## Reasoning

Excellent progress! Score dropped from 7989 to 3439. The champion is:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue) → if on black floor (p=0.9), go to Stop
- State 1: Stop → if gray floor (p=0.8) go back to GoToColor; if random (p=0.02) go back; if few neighbors (below 1, w=5) go back

The "below neighbor threshold" transition in Stop is interesting - if isolated (below 1 neighbor), robot goes back to GoToColor to find others. This encourages aggregation!

**Key improvements to try:**
1. Tune p1x2 threshold (currently 1 neighbor) - maybe 2 neighbors would be better
2. Tune steepness w1x2 (currently 5) 
3. Tune the gray floor probability (0.8)
4. Tune black floor probability (0.9)
5. Add attraction in the strip to pull robots together
6. Try 3-state: GoToColor → Attract (on black) → Stop (when enough neighbors)
7. Reduce p1x1 (random reset, currently 0.02)

## Reasoning

Small improvement from 3439 to 3298. The champion is nearly identical to before but with p1x0=0.75 (gray→GoToColor, was 0.8) and p1x2=2 neighbors threshold (was 1), w1x2=8 (was 5). So:
- Stop → GoToColor if gray floor (p=0.75)
- Stop → GoToColor if random (p=0.02) 
- Stop → GoToColor if fewer than 2 neighbors (w=8) - isolated robots re-explore

The neighbor threshold of 2 with steepness 8 means isolated robots go back to find the group. This is the key aggregation mechanism.

**Exploitation:**
- Try p1x0 values around 0.75 (0.65, 0.70, 0.80)
- Try neighbor thresholds of 2,3 with different steepness
- Try p0x0 (black floor detection) around 0.9 (0.85, 0.95)
- Try p1x1 (random reset) values (0.01, 0.03)
- Try w1x2 values (6, 10, 12)

**Exploration:**
- 3-state: GoToColor → Attract (aggregate on black) → Stop
- Add attraction in stop state when few neighbors
- Try FlockCenter instead of Stop

## Reasoning

New champion at 3125! The 3-state machine adds an Attraction state:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue) → if black floor (p=0.9) go to Stop; if >5 neighbors (w=10) go to Attraction
- State 1: Stop → if gray floor (p=0.75) go to GoToColor; if random (p=0.02) go to GoToColor; if <2 neighbors (w=8) go to GoToColor
- State 2: Attraction(2.0) → if black floor (p=0.85) go to GoToColor; if gray floor (p=0.7) go to Stop

The Attraction state activates when many neighbors are detected - pulling robots together. The logic is:
- GoToColor finds the blue zone
- Stop stays there
- Attraction aggregates when a crowd forms
- Isolated robots return to search

**Exploitation:** Tune parameters:
- p0x0 (0.85, 0.95), p0x1 neighbor threshold (4,6), w0x1 steepness (8,12)
- p1x0 (0.65,0.80), p1x2 threshold (1,3), att2 gain (1.5,2.5,3.0)
- p2x0 (0.80,0.90), p2x1 (0.6,0.8)

**Exploration:**
- 4-state variants
- Different attraction gains
- FlockCenter instead of Attraction

## Reasoning

New champion at 3048! The 4-state machine:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue) → if black floor (p=0.9) go to Stop(1); if >5 neighbors (w=10) go to Attract(2)
- State 1: Stop → if gray floor (p=0.75) go to GoToColor(0); if random (p=0.02) go to GoToColor(0); if <2 neighbors (w=8) go to Attract(2) [destination index 2 = state 2]
- State 2: Attract(2.0) → if >5 neighbors (w=10) self-loop? No - destination 2 from state 2 means state 3; if black floor (p=0.85) go to GoToColor(0); if gray floor (p=0.7) go to Stop(1)
- State 3: Stop → if gray floor (p=0.75) go to GoToColor(0); if <2 neighbors (w=8) go to Attract(2) [destination 2 from state 3 = state 2 (skipping state 3)]

Wait, let me re-read destination indexing: "With 4 states, leaving state X, others are indexed excluding X". So from state 2: destinations [0,1,3] → D=0→state0, D=1→state1, D=2→state3. And from state 3: destinations [0,1,2] → D=0→state0, D=1→state1, D=2→state2.

So state 2 transitions: D=2→state3 (Stop when >5 neighbors), D=0→state0 (GoToColor when black floor), D=1→state1 (Stop when gray).
State 3: D=0→state0 (GoToColor when gray), D=2→state2 (Attract when <2 neighbors).

This is a rich machine. **Exploitation:** tune parameters around the champion values.

## Reasoning

Final round! Champion at 2928.02 - very similar to previous champion but att1=2.0 (was 1.5). The dual-attraction topology is clearly strong:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue) → black floor→Attract(2.0)[state1]; many neighbors→Attract(3.0)[state2]
- State 1: Attract(2.0) → gray→GoToColor(0); random→GoToColor(0); few neighbors→Attract(3.0)[state2]
- State 2: Attract(3.0) → many neighbors→Stop(3); black floor→GoToColor(0); gray→Attract(2.0)[state1]
- State 3: Stop → gray→GoToColor(0); few neighbors→Attract(3.0)[state2]

This is the last round, so I need to be both precise in exploitation AND bold in exploration to find something better.

**Key parameters to tune:**
- att1: try 1.5, 2.5 around 2.0
- att2: try 2.5, 3.5, 4.0 around 3.0
- p0x0 (black→attract1): 0.85, 0.95
- p2x1 (black→GoToColor in attract2): 0.80, 0.90
- Neighbor thresholds: p0x1=4,6; p2x0=4,6; p1x2=1,3; p3x1=1,3
- w steepness: 8→10, 10→12

**Bold explorations:**
- State 3 as Attract(2.0) instead of Stop
- Add 4th transition to state 1 or 2
- Try FlockCenter in state 2

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
